I have the honour to present the legislative initiative of the President of the Republic of Poland, Mr Andrzej Duda - a draft law amending the Law on the Supreme Court. The changes proposed by your President stem from the experience of the application of the Law of 8 December 2017 on the Supreme Court without a small period of 3 years after its entry into force. The main objective of the proposed law is to extend the deadline for lodging an emergency complaint referred to in Article. 115 § 1 of the Supreme Court Act. The emergency complaint appeared in the Polish legal order... [[Game in the O.R.]] [[The meeting is chaired by Deputy Marshal of the Sejm Włodzimierz Czarzasty]] Excuse me for a moment. Ladies and gentlemen, good morning. I would like to remind you that the Sejm is in session, the rapporteur has a voice, and I would ask us not to talk, and those people who want to talk, because it is human - to do so outside the room. You're very welcome. There you go. Secretary of State at the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland Margaret Paprock: Thank you, Marshal. As I mentioned, the emergency complaint appeared in the Polish legal order at the initiative of President Andrzej Duda, which was due to the belief that judgments of the courts should be fair and given on the basis of properly reconstructed legal standards, as well as to correspond to properly collected and evaluated evidence. As needed by the institution, the case law of the Supreme Court is based on the case law of the case-law of the case-law of the Supreme Court. As a general rule, the Act provides that an emergency action shall be brought within 5 years of the judgment under appeal and, if an appeal has been brought against the judgment, within one year of the date of its examination. On the other hand, in accordance with the transitional provision in Article. 115 for a period of 3 years from the date of entry into force of the Act, an emergency complaint may be brought against final decisions which have become valid after 17 October 1997, i.e. after the date of entry into force of the Constitution. An emergency complaint may be brought under such circumstances either by the Attorney General or by the Ombudsman. The extension of the deadline, which will be exemplified on 3 April this year, is driven primarily by facts, i.e. pending cases, as well as the fact that requests for emergency complaints are still being received by the rightholders. Undoubtedly, the objective premise and circumstance is also to make it difficult for the citizens concerned to request emergency complaints due to the pandemic situation. High Izbo! The proposed proposal also aims to amend the Article. 15 Act to supplement the procedures for selecting candidates for the post of President of the Supreme Court - by determining the quorum required to be selected at the meeting of the Judges of the Supreme Court. The proposal also extends the reference to the proper application of the rules on candidates and their selection with regard to the first President of the Supreme Court for candidates and the selection of candidates for the position of President of the Supreme Court. The proposed amendment aims to ensure that the President of the Supreme Court can be elected, which, in view of the statutory duties and powers of the Supreme Court's Presidents, is important for the proper organisation of the work of the various Supreme Court chambers. In the revision of the article. 83 of the Law on the Supreme Court, the President proposes that, in the event of a request for a decision on a legal matter to the Supreme Court, if the caselaw of the general courts, military courts or Supreme Court reveals discrepancies in the interpretation of the law and there is a need to ensure uniformity of case law - that is, the proposal is not related to a specific case under consideration by the Supreme Court - to the allocation of such an application and to the appointment of a composition, as well as to the order in which the application is dealt with, the provisions laid down in Article should apply accordingly. 80 of the Supreme Court Act, except that if the application was submitted by the first President of the Supreme Court or one of the eligible entities, e.g. The Advocate General, the Ombudsman, the Ombudsman, the President of the Prosecutor's Office are the duties and powers of the President of the Supreme Court as regards the allocation of cases and the appointment of the formation of the Judge, as well as the order in which applications are dealt with should be exercised by the first President of the Supreme Court. The draft also proposes the addition of an article. 96a, i.e. a provision (the bell) necessary for the formal regulation of the manner in which the judicial files are created, processed and archived or destroyed in cases recognised by the Supreme Court. The project complements the lack of existing legislation in this area. It should be pointed out that a separate case file is being drawn up in the Supreme Court, independent of the case files before general courts or military courts. Minister, we're almost done. Let the Ombudsman have it. No. The President's office washes its hands of this. Today I repeat my appeal. If we can see that there are blockages, let the President's Office engage in this resolution and the submission of emergency complaints. The President's law firm washes hands of this problem, extending only its duration. That is why I am saying that this is a false image, because the real intention of this presidential project is not an extraordinary complaint at all, but a personal change in the Supreme Court. Yes, sir. We remember the election of the first president of the Supreme Court in the previous term. The emissaries of power, Judge Zaradkiewicz, Judge Stępkowski, conducted in huge chaos the election of the first President of the Supreme Court, whose election and legitimacy are questioned today. And that is why the President now, in his draft bill, proposes that these solutions which failed in the election of the first President of the Supreme Court, be moved to the issue of the Presidents of all chambers. We're running out of terms, so it's an opportunity for the authorities to jump to their posts. That's all this bill is about. An emergency complaint, as I repeat, is just a smokescreen. It will be the President who will have the power to designate his Commissioners in the Supreme Court, i.e. those acting as President of the Chamber. And I'm asking if there's a presidential prerogative in the article. 144 constitutions. Could you point her out, please? There isn't. If there isn't, then such a decision will require a countersignation from the President of the Council of Ministers. What's the effect? Such that the Prime Minister of the Polish Government, the politician, a member of the political party will indicate the persons who perform their duties in the Supreme Court. Isn't that the politicization of the Supreme Court? This is a pure form of politicalization of the Supreme Court. Why is this happening? That is because the Supreme Court is dealing with cases such as the status of judges selected by the politicised National Judicial Council, because it is the Supreme Court that has the power to make binding interpretations when there is a conflict of laws in the system of law. Yes, the President is even proposing a very dangerous change in this area. Today, when there is a need to resolve a legal dispute that is of relevance to citizens, the Presidents of individual chambers determine the compositions that make such important decisions, and according to the presidential project the first President of the Supreme Court will do so. Why? That's because it's easier when one person can manipulate the assemblies, not individual Presidents of Chambers. We observe in the general courts, in the Constitutional Court the full pathology of the functioning of these institutions, in view of the politicisation of the functions of governors in the courts, the manipulation of formations in common courts and in the Constitutional Court. Now the same pathologies the president will want to bring to the Supreme Court. But, of course, we hear from the Seymary that it's just an emergency complaint. Bullshit. This draft law introduces a full politicisation in the Supreme Court as regards the functioning of the highest organisational positions. That is why we respect the project addressed by the Upper House of the Polish Parliament, the Senate, and of course we express our will to work on it, while we believe that the presidential bill in its entirety deserves to be rejected for the reasons mentioned by me. ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u51	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending the Act - Road Traffic Law, which is included in print No 911, regulates the mobility of scooters and other personal transport equipment electrically driven. In the streets of big cities, especially when it is weathering, this has been happening in particular for 2 years, we are dealing with a large number of people who use e-Hulajnog. At the moment, there are no rules to determine how and where you can drive an electric scooter. This state of affairs causes uncontrolled behaviour of users of these devices, behaviours that are very often dangerous for other road users, especially pedestrians. Therefore, the primary objective of the Ministry of Infrastructure in the work on this law is to ensure the safety of pedestrians, to make pedestrians feel safe on the sidewalks and to be sure that they have the right to free, unfettered, unlimited use of pavements. It is the user of e-hulaipodi who will be obliged by the provisions of this law to comply with certain behaviours, he will have to adapt to them. On the other hand, the second objective is to allow users of electric scooters to use them freely, but under certain conditions. We want to achieve these goals, qualifying e-hulaipods, as well as other electrically driven devices as vehicles. This is a fundamental issue, because the driver of the vehicle must have strict obligations, and a number of prohibitions affect it, e.g. No alcohol-driving, no clinging to other vehicles while driving, no two-person driving on one scooter, no leaving the vehicle in an unauthorised place or no use by persons under 10 years of age. Another important change is the definition of infrastructure on which electric scooters can be used. In the first place they are cycle paths, in the second place it is a road on which the maximum permissible speed is 30 km/h, and in the third place it is a pavement, but provided that the place and priority of the pedestrian is given and provided that the speed is close to the pedestrian speed. In our proposal, in our bill we share, we also show devices powered electronically, we divide devices powered electronically into e-hulaipods and personal transport devices. Personal transport devices are e-skateboards, e.g. self-leveling devices. This second category of vehicles is excluded from driving on the road, for them bicycle paths and sidewalks are reserved. The Act allows the use of both electric scooters and personal transport equipment for persons between 10 and 18 years of age, but provided that they have a bicycle card, the same provision exists for cyclists and determines the maximum speed of driving of these vehicles, i.e. 20 km/h. The bill was very strongly and very thoroughly consulted with many environments. It is worth mentioning that a positive opinion on the project in this shape was issued by a joint committee of government and local government. This means that local government officials, especially large cities, those cities in which electric scooters are used, renting them, i.e. using them directly on the street, have adopted these regulations with some relief, consider these regulations to be good, appropriate, beneficial ones, which on the one hand will secure pedestrians and on the other hand will allow the use of electric scooters. We see great interest, especially in the young part of Polish society, [[♪ The bell ♪]] this means of transport, so we do not limit this possibility, and we create the conditions for using e-Hulajnog and other personal transport facilities. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u53	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I think I'm not the only one who's imagining your marshal on this scooter right now. Let spring come as soon as possible. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of our club regarding print no.911, i.e. the draft law amending the Law on Road Traffic and some other laws. The purpose of the government's draft law - Road traffic law and some other laws is to improve pedestrian safety by regulating the legal status of electrically powered equipment and muscle-powered equipment which are intended to move on public roads. Up to now, the legal status of electrically powered equipment and muscle-powered equipment has remained unregulated, creating a real threat to the safety of both pedestrians and users of these devices using sidewalks and roadways. The law provided a pedestrian's preference to electric scooters, personal transport equipment and traffic support devices, which move in part of the infrastructure intended for pedestrian traffic, will oblige the drivers of these vehicles and devices, among other things, to give priority to pedestrians, not hinder them, and secondly, to drive at a speed close to pedestrian speed and, thirdly, to take special care. The proposed solutions are intended in particular to ensure the protection of pedestrians on pavements or pedestrian roads. The bill, first of all, defines the concept of electric scooter separately as an electric, two-axis, steering wheel, without seat and pedals, second, defines personal transport devices, among others. such as electric skateboards, electric self-leveling devices, thirdly, also defines motion aids, i.e. rollers, roller skates and skateboards. At the same time, the proposed law introduces a new type of infringement in the law - Code of Offences. Therefore, the control authorities will be able to impose a fine for travelling by a sidewalk or a pedestrian road as indicated above at a speed greater than the speed close to pedestrian speed or not giving priority to pedestrians. The driving of an electric scooter or a personal transport device by persons aged between 10 and 18 years will require the same rights as for cycling: a bicycle card or a driving licence of categories AM, A1, B1 or T. In the case of persons aged 18 years or over, such a document shall not be required. On the other hand, moving with a muscle-powered motor will not require any additional rights and will be allowed without age restrictions. In addition, the draft law introduces regulations which prohibit, among others. driving an electric scooter or a personal transport device to a person in a state of drunkenness or in a state of drinking alcohol or a medium acting like alcohol, carrying an electric scooter or a personal transport device of other persons, animals and objects, drawing or towing an electric scooter or a personal transport device of other vehicles, or leaving an electric scooter or a personal transport device on the sidewalk, other than a designated place, other than in parallel to its outer edge. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Gentlemen of Ministers! I would like to inform you that Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this bill in the further course of Parliament's work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u63	Thank you, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Since these devices appeared on Polish streets, they have been treated as puppies: they have moved without restrictions on pavements and pedestrian roads, and for driving them under the influence of alcohol there has been no penalty. The inclusion of this last offence in the Code of Misdemeanors is the first of the solutions that Poland 2050 welcomes with great satisfaction. Poland 2050 also welcomes 20 km/h as the maximum speed for electric scooters and personal transport equipment. However, doubt is raised by a record limiting their speed on the sidewalk to a speed similar to that of a pedestrian. Although this speed is defined in the justification for about 7 km/h, it may be difficult to enforce this provision in the absence of a clear limitation in the text of the law itself. On the other hand, it is unclear whether the designer, when deciding on such a provision, consulted him with manufacturers or operators of technical equipment. We consulted them on this. For some devices such speed may simply not provide stable driving, and the only possibility will be to drive the device, not to drive at as low speeds as the one envisaged in this project. This is so much more important that the designer decided to let scooters on the sidewalks along the road, at which the permissible speed is more than 30 km/h. This means in practice that, contrary to the many opinions gathered in the consultation in most cities, scooters will continue to ride mostly on the sidewalks, so the rules must be very precise, respectful of pedestrian safety. The draft law creates a new concept, a concept of motion-enhancing devices and movers using them, whose movement is regulated in the same chapter as pedestrian traffic. However, the regulation is incomplete in relation to people who move with mobility aids. In the absence of a sidewalk or a pedestrian road or a pedestrian is not allowed to use the sidewalk, a pedestrian can use the roadway, but a person moving with a movement aid device cannot do so. The question of whether persons using the so-called "use" needs to be clarified first of all. UWR have the right to use the road when there is no road for pedestrians or bikes. It is also unclear whether people moving with UWR can create loudspeakers. Poland 2050, aware of the changes in the cities of Poland and Europe, which resulted in the spread of systems for renting electric scooters, accepts with great disapproval the fact that from the draft law there is no record of the conclusion of contracts with road managers by traffic operators. With such agreements, local authorities would have a real impact on the way equipment rentals operate in their cities, mainly electric scooters, and could identify financial and non-financial aspects of the operation of scooters operators and other equipment in a specific municipality. This is about regulating situations that are burdensome for residents and other users of traffic, e.g. regulating the fleet size and the maximum number of operators, parking zones with the possibility of excluding specific urban areas, such as cultural parks, the creation of sectors for operators using geofencing technology or the obligation of the operator to employ fleet service workers, as well as to receive autonomous city traffic data. It is also worth considering provisions obliging the operator to provide insurance for OC and NNW users of their equipment. If the designer does not choose to do so, the local government could require the operator to have such insurance in the agreement. Increasing the lower age limit for use of unattended electric scooters [[♪ The bell ♪]] and the UTO up to 14 years also seems reasonable. Therefore, Poland 2050 will seek amendments to this project, but in general we will support this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u103	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen of the Ministers! High Izbo! I would like to present a parliamentary bill amending the Act on anti-communist opposition activists and those oppressed for political reasons and some other laws, print No. 907. High Izbo! The proposed bill meets the demands of anti-communist opponents who have been fighting injustice against former activists for years. It is a response to the requests made in the appeal of 141 signatories issued on the eve of the 40th anniversary of the signing of August agreements, whose initiators and authors were Jarosław Maciej Goliszewski, Dr. Grzegorz Kostrzewa-Zorbas and Leszek Stall. This is another bill in the 5-year process of restoring the dignity of anti-communist opposition activists. Only the Government of Law and Justice has balanced the opposition in their powers with the combatants of the war period. Let me remind you that last year a law was passed providing them with pensions, which represent twice the minimum pension. The project under investigation is another important step towards legal and moral redress for people who fought for our freedom. The proposed law amends the following laws: the Act of 20 March 2015 on anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons, the Act of 24 January 1991 on combatants and certain persons who are victims of war repression and post-war period, the Law of 2 September 1994 on the provision of funds and powers to soldiers of substitute military service forced to employ in coal mines, quarrying, uranium ore plants and construction battalions, and the Act of 31 May 1996 on persons deported to forced labour and employed in labour camps by the Third Reich and the Union of Socialist Soviet Republics, and the Law of 16 November 2006 on the provision of cash and rights to civilian blind victims of war activities. High Izbo! The proposed amendments to the legal acts concerned an increase in the income thresholds for single monetary and periodic monetary assistance and the possibility of granting single monetary aid twice a year. They are intended to enable the provision of assistance to a wider group of eligible persons, in particular in cases where a person is affected by adverse events of chance or suffers a significant deterioration of his or her health, which generally entails a significant increase in health care expenditure, care services or the reduction of the effects of a random event, e.g. fire. The rate of monetary assistance to be provided by the Head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons will also be increased, as their current level does not always allow for adequate assistance to cover expenditure such as the purchase of a wheelchair or the adaptation of accommodation to the type of disability. In order to maintain systemic uniformity, the proposed amendment will also address the assistance provided by the head of the office to combatants and oppressed civilian blind victims of war activities, persons deported to forced labour and imprisoned in labour camps by the Third Reich and the Soviet Union and politically repressed soldiers of military replacement service forced to work in coal mines, quarry, uranium ore plants and construction battalions. High Izbo! The law provides a solution on the basis of which anti-communist opposition activists (The Bell) and those repressed for political reasons who remain in employment will be entitled to additional five working days leave. Mr. Marshal, one more minute, because I can't tell you everything. This project is very wide and many people are waiting for this bill. But, Member, relax. Let's go, take it easy. Good. The proposed law introduces very important changes in pensions and this is also the heart of this law. They aim to offset the negative effects of the political repressions of the communist regime and which undermine workers' freedom. People who, as a result of repression for their efforts to regain independence, were forced to stop providing work in the profession they had previously done, generally later took up less paid work. Many years later, this had negative consequences in determining the amount of pension benefits due. Therefore, the introduction of such a new category of premium periods, in conjunction with the solutions proposed in the Act to adopt legal fiction to receive an average salary at that time, although it will partially mitigate the economic effects of the repression. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! There are still many changes to the proposed law. We will discuss them in detail at the meeting of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy. ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u107	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Last year, the Sejm almost unanimously passed a government bill granting anti-communist opposition activists and politically repressed persons compensation if their pension was less than PLN 2400. This was the remedy for the harm that many people have suffered against communism. For this reason, i.e. during periods of imprisonment, internment, job deprivation, not only had a severely reduced standard of living in times of repression, but received much lower benefits after retirement or pension. Today we're going one step further. We want to fill another gap in the provision of pensions and pensions of people who were committed to communism in favour of Polish sovereignty and political and trade union rights, paying for it often with the status of a citizen and a second-class worker. Both today's draft laws, both senate and print No 578, adopted unanimously by the Upper House and by the Parliamentary Chamber, print No 907, introduced by the Parliamentary Club Group Law and Justice, provide for equalisation of contributions to pension insurance for periods of imprisonment, internment and employment for political organisations and trade unions illegal in the period of the Polish People's Republic, and for periods of non-work and deprivation of the possibility of exercising their profession as a result of political repression by calculating these contributions from the level of average remuneration of those periods. In our opinion, the parliamentary project introduces a better solution. The aim is to establish initial capital using contributions calculated from average remuneration for these periods of repression. It's going to be about 5,000. persons and annual expenditure on increased pension benefits is estimated at about PLN 10, 11 million. The Parliamentary project also introduces in the Act on activists of anti-communist opposition, the Act on Combatants and other laws concerning soldiers of military substitute service forced to work in mines and quarryes, persons deported to forced labour and prisoners in labour camps by the Third Reich and the USSR, as well as civil blind victims of war changes which increase income thresholds, entitle to one-off and periodic monetary assistance and enable the granting of one-off assistance twice a year. The Parliamentary bill also provides for the anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for political reasons remaining in employment to be entitled to additional leave of 5 working days per calendar year. The draft Group of Members of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice also amends the Law on the provision of money to persons sent or deported by the Soviet Union authorities in the years 1936-1956, allowing grant this benefit to a higher dimension of 1100 persons for whom repression started before 1956 and continued thereafter and granting this benefit to 90 persons for whom repression started after 1956. These are the most common people born on exile. The cost of payment of these benefits will be about PLN 2.5 million. All these solutions are very expected by the environment's anti-communist activists and oppressed people. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice strongly supports these solutions and advocates the transfer of both projects to committee work. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

[[The Gathered Rise Up]] The resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland in connection with the 30th anniversary of the creation of the Visegrad Group of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland expresses its appreciation and deep gratitude for the fundamental political act of inaugurating the cooperation of the Visegrad Group on 15 February 1991. 30 years ago, the presidents of Poland and Czechoslovakia Lech Wałęsa and Václaw Havel and Hungarian Prime Minister József Antall held a historic meeting in Visegrad, which gave rise to regional cooperation between Central European countries for security, development and European and Euro-Atlantic integration. Our joint efforts resulted in accession to the North Atlantic Pact of Poland, the Czech Republic and Hungary in 1999 and Slovakia in 2004. In the same year 2004 our countries' accession procedures to the European Union were successful. The implementation of these strategic objectives confirmed the effectiveness of the joint action, reflecting on the fact that Visegrad Group countries should operate within the EU and NATO as a regional community of close neighbours and friends. Soon we could prove that our membership in these structures involves their active co-formation. Our participation in the discussion on security issues led to the decision to set up the eastern NATO flank, and activity in the EU - among others. on the future of the EU, its enlargement, its neighbourhood policy, migration policy or in discussions on the shape of cohesion policy within the framework of the multiannual financial framework - it has protected the interests of Central Europe. The parliamentary dimension of the Visegrad Group is particularly important for deepening regional cooperation. Undisputed status has been achieved by meetings of the Presidents of the V4 parliaments, which are often attended by partners from other EU countries. Cooperation within the sectoral committees and the Parliamentary rapid response team on subsidiarity control is important. From the perspective of three decades we appreciate the strength of our community, thanks to which we have gained protection of regional interests and international recognition. Our cooperation is a combination of historical and mentally close efforts of nations, in the past of similarly hard-experienced and similar visions and programmes of their own development. On the 30th anniversary of the inauguration of Visegrad cooperation, we express our highest respect for the achievements of our countries, societies and parliaments and declare the continuation of our efforts to integrate and develop our community. ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u168	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. First, Mr Andrzej Noblet, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u169	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law amending the law - Bank law and some other laws, print No 859. The aim of this project is to amend banking law as a result of the introduction by the European Union of the rules on capital requirements for capital institutions contained in the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 May 2019, referred to as the CRD V Directive, and in the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 May 2019, also referred to as Regulation CRR II. The CRD V Directive amend Directive 2013/36/EU as regards exempt entities, financial holding companies, mixed financial holding companies, remuneration, supervisory measures and powers and capital protection measures. CRR II amends Regulation No 575/2013 with regard to leverage ratio, net stable financing ratio, own funds and eligible liabilities requirements, counterparty credit risk, market risk, exposures to central counterparties, exposures to collective investment undertakings, large exposures, reporting and disclosure requirements. CRR V and CRR II are elements of the legislative package including the revision of the 2014 Directive as regards the ability to cover losses and recapitalisations of credit institutions and investment firms and the revision of the 2014 Regulation as regards the ability to cover losses and recapitalisations of credit institutions and investment firms. These changes were the result of the financial crisis in 2007 and 2008 and reflect internationally agreed standards. The draft law also contains provisions supplementing the transposition of the Directives of the European Parliament and of the Council of 2009 and 2013 on conditions for the admission of credit institutions to the business and prudential supervision of credit institutions and investment firms. These amendments require amendments to the following laws in Polish law: Banking Law of 29 August 1997, the Act on the Functioning of Cooperative Banks of December 2000, the Act on Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego of 2003, the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments of 29 July 2005, the Act on Supervision of the Financial Market of July 2006, the Act on Macroprudential Supervision of the Financial System and Crisis Management in the Financial System of August 2015, the Act on Insurance and Reinsurance Activities of September 2015 and the Act on Banking Guarantee Fund, the Deposit Guarantee Scheme and the Forced Restructuring of June 2016. The regulations contained in this proposal will not affect the activities of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. They won't affect the labour market. The entry into force of this law will not affect the situation and regional development. The evaluation of the effects of the bill will be carried out on an ongoing basis by the Financial Supervision Commission. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this bill in further legislative work. On behalf of the club, I submit six amendments to this bill. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u170	Thank you very much. Mr Wojciech Saluga, Club of the Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u171	Thank you, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The project concerns the implementation of an EU reform to amend the regulation on capital requirements for financial institutions, i.e. Articles 4 (1) point (b) and 4 (1) point (b) of CRR. The proposed changes will result, among others. the extension of the powers of the Commission for Financial Supervision to approve the conduct of these financial institutions' activities. In the project appears e.g. the possibility of ordering banks to comply with the requirement to hold additional own funds with a higher share of capital or core capital. The project will replace the liquidation of the cooperative bank where the bank's own funds fall below the regulatory minimum, i.e. from EUR 1 million by taking over the cooperative bank by another bank. This project also makes it possible to authorise the creation of a new bank subject to the possession of a risk management system and internal control system, as well as to include a scheme of the organisational structure of the group to which the applicant belongs. The draft shall also take into account judicial administrative case-law regarding the procedural legitimacy of a candidate as a member of the board of directors of a supervised entity in the event of an appeal against a decision refusing to act by that candidate. High Izbo! We must realise that this bill, this change is the result of the financial crisis in 2007 and 2008 and a rather late response to this crisis. The Directive is from 2019 and has been adopted before the current crisis and before the introduction of so-called policies across Europe. zero interest rates, which is undoubtedly affecting the financial condition of banks. Banks are losing their profits, profitability is decreasing. Let us hope that these changes will help to make it easier for banks to survive the current times and current government policies. ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u174	Thank you. Mr Jacek Protasiewicz, Coalition Poland.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u175	Mr. Marshal! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, The draft amendment to the Banking Law is a very extensive document, because apart from the law itself, the bank itself, this law proposes to amend seven other laws, including the Act on the functioning of cooperative banks, their association and the banks of associations, the Act on Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments, the Law on Financial Market Supervision, the Act on Macro-prudential Supervision of Financial System and Crisis Management in Financial System, the Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities, as well as the Act on Bank Guarantee Fund, the Deposit Guarantee Scheme and the Forced Restructuring. As a result, the proposed changes will affect at least 30 commercial and state banks, 535 cooperative banks and 35 branches of foreign banks. It is therefore not only a comprehensive document, but also a very important document. It was created as a result of the need to transfer to the Polish legal order four EU regulations already adopted by Parliament and the Council of the European Union more than 18 months ago. They consist of a legislative package, which is the result of the reform carried out by the European Union. This involves changing the regulatory framework for financial services provided within the Community. These changes respond to the financial crisis that broke out in Europe in 2007 and 2008. They reflect the wider than only the EU's arrangements, because they were developed in a wider group than the EU, on an international dimension, of the global financial market. In practice, they stem from the introduction into national legal systems of new provisions concerning the approval of mixed financial holding companies and mixed financial holding companies, which may be the parent undertakings of groups of capital, banking groups, and therefore it is necessary to apply prudential requirements to them, since so far these companies have not been subject to adequate financial supervision. The second major innovation is the holding of an EU intermediary entity by a third country group, provided that at least two institutions established in the European Union are part of such a group and the total value of assets in the European Union of that third country group is equivalent to EUR 10 billion, EUR 40 billion or more. This will enable the financial supervisory authority in one of the EU countries to supervise the activities of such a large group. The new regulations which are introduced in the framework of this amendment also give the Commission's financial supervision authority to dismiss a member of the management board of the bank, a financial holding company and a mixed financial holding company as well as a member of the management board of the brokerage house where such a person does not meet the requirements necessary to perform the function in question. The project discussed today was already the subject of work at first reading at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance. As a result, a number of amendments have been introduced, e.g. clarified the rules on remuneration applied by banks and brokerage houses which are not large institutions, which we believe, in the opinion of the Polish Coalition Club, is moving in the desired direction, i.e. towards the application of the principle of proportionality, and consequently reducing reporting and administrative obligations in the case of smaller financial institutions. As a consequence, the amendments adopted by the Public Finance Commission on banks and brokerage houses are also to be required to provide information to the NCF on gender pay differentials, which, in our view, is also a step in the right direction. Similarly, we assess the amendment proposed by the Committee on Public Finance which gives the rigour of the finality and immediate feasibility of the decision to impose a fine on the persons responsible and the members of supervisory boards and credit institutions, as well as those which clarify the provisions on suspicion of crime and the risk of committing a crime in the activities of a bank, financial holding company or mixed financial holding company. Given that we are dealing mainly with the implementation of EU law, which is due to expire because the time for implementing directives and regulations was 18 months from May 2019 and that these solutions are going in the right direction, the Polish Coalition club is in favour of adopting this law. [[♪ The bell ♪]] We will, of course, look at the amendments that have been tabled, announced today by the Law and Justice Club. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u195	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the draft law amending the Anti-Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Act and some other laws. The draft law presented in print No 909 aims to implement Directive 2018/843 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 30 May 2018 amending previous Union legislation and clarifies the provisions of the existing Law of 1 March 2018. The changes included in the project include: clarification of the list of obliged institutions, including by adding to it traders engaged in trading or intermediation in the trading of works of art, collectors' items and antiques, as well as storage, trading or intermediation in the trading of such goods. These are transactions with a value equal to or exceeding the equivalent of 10 000. the euro regardless of whether this transaction is carried out as a single operation or several operations which appear to be linked. The project contains details of some definitions, including: the beneficial owner, the Member State and the group. It shall include an extension of the scope of the statistics collected by the General Financial Information Officer and a clarification of the rules on the application by the institutions of the financial security measures required, as well as their action on high risk relations with third countries. It shall specify the rules for the institution's retention of documents and information resulting from the application of financial security measures. It introduces an obligation to publish and update a list of public posts and functions which, under national law, can be classified as politically exposed positions. It introduces mechanisms for verifying the data contained in the Central Register of Actual Recipients. It introduces the obligation to register currency exchange service providers between virtual and fiducional currencies. All these solutions are designed to prevent money laundering and terrorist financing, so our club, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, supports this government bill and recommends that it be adopted by the High House. At the same time, I am proposing two amendments clarifying this draft law and ask for this draft law to be adopted together with the amendments. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u197	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The draft law we are talking about has been consulted with organisations bringing together the required institutions, among others. banks and non-bank financial institutions. It introduces e.g. a broader catalogue of obliged institutions, new powers and duties of the General Inspector for Financial Information, changes in the field of identification and verification of the client and the beneficial owner as well as new registers. The rapporteur has already mentioned this. I would add that most of the proposed provisions are indeed due to the need to regulate the EU directive into the Polish legal order, and it is clear that these provisions are intended to build an appropriate coherent mechanism not only in the Member States but also in some way coherent across Europe, and therefore absolutely need to be supported. However, I have some comments I would like to make on behalf of the Civic Coalition Club. The first point concerns close to an annual delay in this implementation. The project should have been adopted on 10 January 2020 and we are only leaning on it now. I asked this at the meeting of the finance committee, and as far as I understand the difficulties resulting from the transposition of Community law, often based on common law, into Polish order, so slowly such delays in the implementation of EU directives have already become a tradition of this government. Even if this does not always result in penalties on the part of the European Union, it almost always results in hasty, incomplete consultations, the writing of law on the knee, a lot of amendments made during the legislative process. This really is not needed and I am allergic once again and I ask the government to be more disciplined in this respect, especially in the case of such complex and complex matter and involving a multitude of actors. My second point concerns consultation and, in principle, their effect. The consultation was indeed quite broad, but unfortunately you have not decided to take into account some of the most reasonable amendments tabled by the participants in the consultation. I am talking about provisions which are clearly excessive in relation to the directive and impose additional obligations on institutions. As an example, let us refer to the provision that obliged institutions should apply financial security measures to customers with whom they maintain economic relations, in particular. where pre-established customer or beneficial owner data have been changed. In this way, even a change in the address of the beneficial owner will require a re-verification of the customer. This was noted, for example. The Legislative Council, as well as the Confederation of Leviathan. However, in the expert opinion only key changes, such as, for example, concerning the ownership structure, should consequently lead to a re-analysis of the client's risk. The project also assumes the introduction of a mechanism for verifying the data contained in the Central Register of Recipients by increasing the responsibilities of the obliged institutions. The institution will be required to record discrepancies between the information collected in the CRBR and the information on the actual customer's beneficiary established by it and to take steps to explain the reasons for those discrepancies. Then, in case of identification of discrepancies, the institution required to provide the CRBR with the verified information, together with the justification and the relevant documentation. Such a wording of the proposed provision de facto transfers the obligation to ensure that the data contained in the CRBR are reliable and accurate from the State to the institutions. I am not sure that this was the intention expressed in the implementing directive, especially since the institutions are obliged to have different, sometimes very limited, possibilities to verify data contained in CRBR, video e.g. Notaries on a one-off transaction. This is so important that countering terrorist financing and money laundering in recent years is becoming not only a method of preventing crime, but also a powerful tool for monitoring the lives of citizens, as well as a source of extremely burdensome responsibilities for entrepreneurs. Therefore, the Civic Coalition Club, in support of the proposed novel in principle, hopes that in the course of further work on the project, Members will continue to lean on these details so as to remove excess obligations from the project and make this necessary project more friendly to the institutions bound. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u200	Thank you. Mr Jacek Protasiewicz, Coalition Poland.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u201	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The draft amendment to the Anti-Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Act and some of the other laws that we are currently discussing is another project submitted by the government resulting from the change of EU law. This concerns in particular the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 30 November 2018. This is a directive on securing the use of the financial system for money laundering or terrorist financing. The direct reason for the changes to this regulation was the analysis of financial flows to finance the terrorist attacks that took place several years ago in France and Belgium, as well as information disclosed by the international journalistic investigation on the so-called "news" case. Panama Papers. The clear objective of the changes proposed by the European Union was to ensure greater transparency of financial flows and thus to increase the effectiveness of the detection of funds from criminal activities, in particular measures to finance terrorist actions. So what does the European Union propose? Firstly, to clarify the rules on actions taken by economic operators having relations with high risk countries outside the European Union, secondly, to improve the execution of transactions carried out using virtual currencies, thirdly, to limit the use of anonymous prepaid instruments, fourthly, to provide law enforcement authorities with rapid access to information on bank and payment account holders and, fifthly, to increase transparency in the area of information on beneficial owners of real corporate entities and legal arrangements. On the other hand, how do these recommendations, and in principle the European Union is very firm on this issue - want to implement the Polish government in the proposed amendment of the law? First of all, the government proposes to extend the so-called list. obliged institutions, i.e. private entities, which are obliged to cooperate with public authorities in typing suspicious transactions and informing the General Financial Information Inspector thereof. Thus, in the light of the amended law, entrepreneurs engaged in trade in or mediation in works of art, collectors and antiques will also be included in the current list if they carry out transactions equal to or exceeding the equivalent of 10 000. The euro, and it doesn't have to be a one-off transaction, it could be a few transactions that give the impression that they're connected. In addition, the amendment extends the scope of statistics collected by the General Financial Information Inspector. The new Act also clarifies the principles of financial security, especially for entrepreneurs, those who are included in the list of obliged institutions, in relations with countries, with entities, with partners from third countries, from outside the European Union, especially from high risk countries. The Act, this amendment also requires the registration of virtual currency exchange service providers and virtual currency account providers, and also provides - more so for all of us here in the Chamber - the obligation to publish and update the selection of public positions and functions that qualify as prominent political positions. The scope of the new regulations is very wide, because it appeals... However, it affects not only new economic operators, but also, as I have already said, especially companies involved in the marketing and marketing of works of art, antiques and collectors' items. In principle, it covers about 650 financial institutions, including banks, credit institutions, as well as cooperative savings and credit banks. In the course of the Commission's first reading work, amendments were tabled to provide the general inspector with financial information on the central system of information on bank accounts and accounts held in cooperative savings and credit banks, and in our opinion these amendments are acceptable, which is why the Polish Coalition Club will support the project submitted and is ready for further work on its completion. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The question of banking tax is not the subject of a general statement by the Financial Information Inspector and even the bill has not been amended by the Act on Tax on Certain Financial Institutions. With regard to issues related to the international approach, this is the international FAT institution which creates such global guidelines, good practices, rules for the functioning of AML cells on a global scale, not only in the European Union. As has been said here, these are solutions coming from more American than European law, so that there were also some difficulties in implementing the law. I can say that countries such as: Belgium, the Netherlands or Austria have already been sued for not implementing the AML rules. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u216	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. First, Mr Andrzej Noblet, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u217	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! I would like to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law amending the Customs Law and some other laws. As I pointed out at the first reading of this draft law, the main objective of the amendment is to simplify and streamline the regulation of customs and tax obligations and the fuel levy and issue fee for imports of goods. The amendment is intended to reduce the formalities relating to the import of goods, including the reporting obligation. The main changes include: appointment of the Head of the National Tax Administration in place of the Minister responsible for these matters, deformisation of the person's entry on the list of customs agents, appointment of the head of the customs and treasury office in place of the head of the tax office as the competent authority for the acceptance of information on the issue fee, clarification of the provisions of the excise duty Act in respect of imports of goods, deformisation, simplification and shortening of the procedure for carrying out gambling cases, harmonisation of customs and treasury controls, the possibility for the head of the customs and treasury office to apply the issue fee in an administrative execution procedure. During the work of the Committee on Public Finance and the detailed discussion of this draft law, minor changes were made to the agreement with editorial legislators, which is why Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the adoption of this draft law in the vote. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u218	Thank you very much. Mr Arkadiusz Carrot, the Civic Coalition, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u219	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! This is, of course, the second reading of the draft law on amending the law - Customs law. We have already had the opportunity to discuss this at the previous meeting of the Committee on Public Finances, but let me once again draw your attention and highlight the most important issues that arise from this debate. The bill concerns changes in three key areas. The first is the right to operate and operate importers. It is worth noting that customs and tax proceedings are currently conducted separately for these issues. Of course, as a result of these proceedings, depending on what kind of documents are issued, there are even several of these decisions. The aim of this project is to condense and make these rules more transparent, which will result in a single decision being taken for the whole of this issue. The second area, of course, includes changes in the fuel and emission charge regulations. At this point, changes have been proposed which adapt the fuel levy rules to the provisions of the excise duty Act and, moreover, give the possibility to apply for reimbursement of the fuel levy. It should be noted here that it will actually be refunded in the cases and on the basis of the arrangements laid down for the refund of excise duty, and this refund will be made at the request of the entity which is entitled to such reimbursement. One more issue concerns technical solutions. It is precisely that, after the proposed legislation is introduced, the head of the customs and treasury office will become the authority that will accept information about the issue fee, of course in the case of imports of motor fuels. Now this information is being submitted to the head of the tax office, so as I mentioned, this is a technical change. The third area, which seems to raise the biggest debate at the committee meeting, includes changes in gambling areas. Firstly, it has been pointed out that today, in the 21st century, these decisions or all the information that they provide should be communicated by electronic means. It's natural today. I believe that the administration should be adapted to the challenges and opportunities of technological development on such issues. Moreover, two amendments were proposed here, which we discussed at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance. The first concerns the amendment of the current law, which indicates that in the place, in the premises where the activity related to slot machines has so far been illegally carried out, it could not have been legalised or carried out for a period of 5 years. You are proposing and the government is proposing to take a picture of this provision. The second point is precisely the change in the need for audiovisual material to be stored by those involved in such activities. In the case of casinos and places where there are slots, the law now refers to 3-year storage of this audiovisual material. This period for casinos is 3 years, while the period for slot machines is reduced to 1 year. We agree that these two provisions, which the government wants to amend, should be in the wording in force, as it is written in the Act, if I remember correctly, from 2017. So, paying attention to the whole and to the legitimacy of these issues, it should be noted that to a large extent this is facilitating procedural issues for entrepreneurs and making this service easier simply because there will be a single procedure, but there are also issues relating to gambling law which we believe are related to the loosening of those requirements which have been applied so far. I make three amendments to the Marshal on these two areas, so that these provisions on these matters concerning gambling regulations do not change, but leave them as they were. Thank you kindly.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u239	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you very much for your questions. If I may, I would like to address in particular the issue raised here of the revision of the law on gambling. Two cases concern the abolition of a 5-year period for limiting the placement of gaming salons in a place where illegal activities were carried out and the shortening of the period of storage of the audio system. It was said here by Mr Carrot that we are loosening up the proposed regulations. We don't loosen up these regulations. In fact, we are adapting to reality and to the conclusions and experiences of observing the functioning of these rules over a number of years. The abolition of the 5-year period only concerns the possibility of placing such lounges for legitimate gaming entities. In fact, it is also an opportunity for persons with premises, i.e. tenants, to freely dispose of such premises. It is also an action aimed at protecting players and allowing them to use legitimate points of the game and limiting the operation of the grey zone and illegal points. In this case, the duty of 3 years to store audiovisual recording is an excess obligation, since in gaming venues the recording of the facts related to playing takes place primarily in systems that record the course of games on slots. And this is the main information and basis for assessing tax obligations. On the other hand, the audio-visual system is an additional control element. As regards opposition, as well as reservations on issues related to slot games, the deadline is 3 months and a period of 12 months is sufficient to allow this additional function of the audio system to be used. As far as slot games are concerned, I would like to remind you that slot machines can only be legally run by a monopoly company of the Treasury, and above all this is important when assessing the five-year condition or the deadline for storing data from the audio system. You have also raised the question of strengthening the KAS in the area of IT systems control equipment and expenditure in this direction. I would like to remind and inform you that a modernization resolution is currently being implemented, which concerns the National Tax Administration. For the period 2021 and 2022, we have the planned resources for the provision of control equipment, for the purchase of IT equipment, as well as the link. So that these purchases and increases in expenditure in recent years are taking place on computerization and equipment of the National Tax Administration. These are significant expenditures. There was also the issue of opening casinos in a day, during a pandemic. Well, I wanted to remind you that casinos, yes, are open during this recent period of slight economic loosening, but alcohol is not allowed in the sanitary regime. With regard to the issue of electronicisation, the use of information systems, these solutions aim primarily at speeding up the transmission of information by gambling operators. This is faster information acquisition and thus facilitating activities. On the other hand, no additional employment is provided for in this Act in connection with the performance of these tasks. Only nine additional posts related to the extension of the scope of the entry into the register of gambling domains are foreseen. Here, the implementation of these tasks is at the level of the authority to be designated, the body authorised by the Head of the KAS. In general, activities in the area of gambling, activities related to the retention of slots, the storage of these illegal slots and any control activities are primarily preventive. This is the protection of citizens, the health of citizens, the protection of young people. [[♪ The bell ♪]] These preventive tasks, regardless of the cost, must be carried out for the benefit of society. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u271	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club regarding the amendment of the Road Transport Act, No. 908 and No. 948. The proposed amendments follow the implementation regulations introduced by Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) No 2017/2005 of 29 November 2017 on detailed rules for the procedures for the technical roadside inspection of commercial vehicles showing serious or dangerous deficiencies. According to the Government's proposal, the Chief Inspector of Road Transport will be responsible for collecting data from road inspections and technical inspections of vehicles from different categories: M2, M3, N2, N3, O3, O4 and T5. These are vehicles for passenger transport, cargo transport, trailers and wheeled tractors. The amendment of the Act will oblige, among others. voivodship police chiefs, Border Guard chiefs or tax administration chambers to provide the Chief Inspector of Road Transport with information about deficiencies detected during the inspection of the above mentioned deficiencies. the categories of vehicles. The bill indicates that, among other things, the VIN number of the controlled vehicle remained in the database and was available in the event of further checks or subsequent infringements. Following the introduction of this law, the Chief Road Transport Inspector will be required to create RSI messages. In addition, the Chief Inspector of Road Transport will be responsible for transmitting information to the European Union countries after detection of defects or failure during the inspection carried out in Poland, when the vehicle will be registered outside the Republic of Poland. The draft law aims to ensure that all the required data during the audit can be collected and processed in order to communicate jointly in the countries of the European Union. The Law and Justice Club, seeing the efforts made by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki and Minister of Infrastructure Andrzej Adamczyk to improve road safety, supports the proposed draft amendment of the bill, which also aims to increase safety on Polish roads. Therefore, the Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of the proposed amendments to the Act. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u274	Thank you very much, sir. Mr Maciej Kopiec, Left. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u275	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Print nr 908 is a draft law on amending the Road Transport Act and is an adaptation of Polish law to European Union legislation. It concerns the procedures for reporting commercial vehicles showing serious or dangerous deficiencies identified during the roadside technical inspection. The aim is to clarify and clarify the provisions already in force relating to the keeping by the Chief Inspector of Road Transport of the central register of infringements. The main objective is to ensure the proper functioning and flow of data between the countries of the European Union and the Member States of the European Free Trade Agreement in accordance with the requirements of the European Commission Regulation of 2017. Central records of infringements are already in place in Poland, and the solution will expand the vehicle VIN number and vehicle category collected in Poland and will further aim at the efficient exchange of messages between contact points located in the European Union and EFTA countries. In practice, this means allowing the transmission of information about the technical inspection carried out and the finding that the vehicle shows serious or dangerous defects to the country where the vehicle was registered in the form of an RSI message. This is another step towards harmonising the rules on technical roadside checks checking the suitability for use of commercial vehicles in the European Union. The optimal solution is for new services to operate in conjunction with the ICT system operated by the Chief Road Transport Inspector. The adjustment would initially cost 600,000. PLN, but after analysis the secured amount was increased by 100,000. zł. Bulgaria and Cyprus also try to do this. Italy shall carry out a national census based solely on administrative sources. Latvia, Estonia, Greece, Malta have taken steps to obtain access to the identification and address data to enable statistical surveys to be carried out. ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u331	Thank you very much. High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on the parliamentary draft law amending the Law amending the Law - Hunting Law, print No 932, committee report, print No 958. The first reading of the project took place at a committee meeting on 23 February this year, followed by a detailed examination of the project. High Izbo! Both the hunting economy and the estimation and payment of hunting compensation have been controversial for years. The work on changes in the Hunting Law in the previous term of the Sejm lasted over a year. Tedious work, consultation with numerous stakeholders led to the adoption of a thorough amendment of the Law of 22 March 2018. A further amendment of these provisions resulted from the Law of 20 December 2019 amending certain laws to facilitate the eradication of certain infectious animal diseases. In accordance with the provisions laid down at that time, Article. Paragraph 7 2 Division of the voivodship into hunting circuits and counting of hunting circuits into individual categories by resolution of the voivodship sejm before the date of entry into force of this statutory amendment shall remain valid until 31 March 2021. Also up to this date, the agreements concluded for the lease of hunting circuits shall apply. In the course of the work on changes to Hunting Law in the previous term, the opinions of the Marshals of voivodships were taken into account in order to extend the deadline for the division of voivodships into hunting circuits due to the complexity of this task, the necessity of multilateral consultations and arrangements, the great amount of work. This deadline was set at 31 March 2021 rather than 31 March 2020. However, due to the pandemic, due to difficulties with the remote completion of the split procedure, sometimes repeated consultations and arrangements with very many entities, the term set out in the Law on Hunting Law was not respected by local governments of voivodships. Therefore, it is necessary to amend the Act of 22 March 2018 and to define new terms for voivodship local governments. This need is being met by the Parliamentary draft law contained in print No 932. In line with the proposed new wording of the Article. Paragraph 7 1 the deadline during which voivodship sejmies are obliged to make new divisions of voivodships into hunting circuits by way of a resolution will be postponed from 31 March 2021 to 31 December 2021. Thus, the divisions of voivodships into hunting circuits and their classification into different categories made prior to the date of entry into force of the amended act will remain valid until 31 March 2022. The lease agreements for hunting circuits in force on the date of entry into force of the amended act will apply until 31 March 2022 in accordance with the amended article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of this Act. An important reference point here is the hunting and economic year beginning on 1 April and ending on 31 March, to which must be applied, adjusted lease agreements for hunting circuits, actually all the activity in this area. Ladies and gentlemen! It is clear that the submission of this deadline raises a lot of controversy, is subject to reasonable criticism, while the task of the responsible legislator is to respond to the state that we are dealing with, and in the discussion that took place in the committee, we talked about the need to decide on disciplinary measures and to mobilise regional governments to fulfil their statutory obligations. However, this is difficult to do in this law, because like any disciplinary measure they must be prosecuted impeccably and after consultation. I think it's gonna happen. High Izbo! In the course of the detailed work on the project, the Environment Committee amended Article 1. 1. In addition, the amendment adopted by the vote introduced the draft Article. 2, referring to the divisions of voivodships into hunting circuits and classifications into particular categories of circuits which were carried out by voivodship seymikas, which were able to comply with the statutory obligation. These divisions will start to apply on 1 April 2022. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Art. 3 provides that the law shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication with effect from 31 March 2021. The draft was adopted by the committee by 11 votes in favour with 8 opponents and 2 abstentions. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, please adopt the draft law contained in the committee's report in print No 958. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u332	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Maria Kurowska, Law and Justice will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u333	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen of the Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the club Law and Justice I have the honour to present the position of the club in the scope of the draft law amending the law on amendment of the law - Hunting Law. The main objective of the law amending the law discussed by the President is to ensure continuity of the conduct in Poland by the tenants and managers of hunting circuits in the hunting economy, the estimation of hunting damage in crops and agricultural fetuses and the payment of compensation due for this, as well as action to combat African swine fever. The direct reason for the legislative work is the threat of the expiry of the existing division of Poland into hunting circuits and existing agreements for the lease of hunting circuits before the new division of voivodships into hunting circuits by seymikas of voivodships and the conduct of the procedure related to the lease or transfer of hunting circuits to the Management Board. The Act of 22 March 2018 amending the Law - Hunting Law and certain other laws obliges in Article. Paragraph 7 1 seymikas of voivodships to be divided into hunting circuits within the voivodship by resolution by 31 March 2021. At the same time, in accordance with the Article. Paragraph 7 2 of this law divisions of voivodships into hunting circuits and the classification of hunting circuits into individual categories made before the date of entry into force of this law shall remain valid until 31 March 2021. By the end of January 2021 resolutions on the division of voivodships into hunting circuits were adopted only in seven voivodships, i.e. Opole, Lubuskie, Lower Silesia, Warmia and Mazury, Pomerania, Świętokrzyskie and Podkarpackie, which I represent. Given the current state of play on draft resolutions on the division of voivodship into hunting circuits in other voivodships, it is not possible to adopt these resolutions in all voivodships until 31 March 2021. Therefore, the Marshal's Office of the Mazowieckie Voivodship and the Marshal's Office of the Małopolska Voivodship requested the Minister of Climate and Environment to undertake work on extending the deadline for the division of voivodships into hunting circuits. The loss of validity of the current division of the voivodship into hunting circuits, while the lack of possibility of re-leasing them due to the failure to make a new division of voivodships into hunting circuits, will lead to a situation in which in part of the territory of the Republic of Poland it will be impossible to conduct a widely understood hunting economy. In particular, it will not be possible to take action to counter the hunting damage caused by the game in crops and agricultural fetuses and their estimation by the tenants of the hunting circuits and their payment of compensation due for this purpose. It will also be impossible for the tenants to manage the hunting circuits with the populations of the game, leading to an uncontrolled increase in the population of individual species, thus translating into the size and value of the damage done in crops and agricultural fetuses, including for wild boars that are the reservoir of the presence of the virus. ASF in the environment. As regards the deadline for the amendment - already mentioned by the President - the seismics are to adopt resolutions on this matter by 31 December. This is an excellent term because it will allow the conclusion of lease contracts before the start of the next marketing year 2022/2023. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The proposed term vacathio legis is also without prejudice to the principle of a democratic rule of law. Thus, the Law and Justice Club supports this bill and will vote for it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

High Izbo! The amendment of the Act concerns regulation and thus the monitoring of the careers of secondary school graduates. The project sets out the legal framework for the newly created, extended system for monitoring the careers of secondary school graduates. Its primary objective is to support the State's education policy on better matching secondary education to the needs of the labour market. A comprehensive and ambitious system for monitoring the careers of secondary school graduates, introduced by the proposed law, will provide analyses that can be used in the development of education policies carried out by governments and local governments both on a regional scale and at district level. The analyses of educational and professional fates of graduates will be used by various actors, as well as students and of course employers. Symbiosis is the basis for functioning. ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u378	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles.
