ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u379	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government's draft law amending the Law - Education Law and some other laws, Parliamentary Printing No. 917. The project is a consequence of changes in the vocational training system made by the government during the previous term of office. The Government's draft law provides primarily for the addition of provisions in the Law of 14 December 2016 - Educational law that comprehensively regulates the monitoring of the careers of secondary school graduates. The draft law defines the objectives of the activities carried out, sets out precisely the scope, forms and periods of use of personal data necessary for monitoring and sets out the legal basis for their processing. The education system, in particular vocational education, should respond to the needs of the labour market, promote lifelong learning and allow graduates to mobility in education and occupation. For this to happen, it is necessary to provide feedback on the career of graduates. Knowledge of graduates' careers makes it possible to respond strategically to the education system and labour market policy in an ever-changing socio-economic reality. Information on the monitoring of secondary school graduates' careers therefore plays an important role in adapting the education system to the needs of the labour market, in order to promote competitiveness and innovation at local, regional but also national level, to combat the shortage of skilled workers and to enable graduates to take up satisfactory employment. The proposed changes will also be important for professional advice and will allow for the use of detailed statistics on the situation of secondary school graduates in the labour market. A reliable and easily accessible knowledge of the careers of graduates will build an image of industry education based on evidence. Access to systematically collected and made available information on industry education will help to overcome the negative stereotypes that affect it, although they do not find confirmation in reality. Information on the labour market situation of graduates of certain types of schools and occupations also has a promotional value and can be used to encourage pupils to continue education in industry education. The monitoring system will allow educational decisions to be taken by school leavers based on reliable information on the impact of this choice rather than another school. In the longer term, this information will help to adapt the offer of schools to the needs of pupils and employers. The career monitoring system for graduates will also provide reliable information on the employment conditions and pay of graduates in particular professions, sectors and regions. Such information significantly reduces information asymmetry in relations between first-time graduates and employers. Employers can use this information to update and better match graduate employment to the realities of the labour market. The draft law is not contrary to European Union law. The draft law does not require the submission to the competent authorities and institutions of the European Union, including the European Central Bank, for the purpose of obtaining an opinion, notification, consultation or agreement. Thus, the government's draft law is an essential condition for the preparation of the system in the proposed form, because the data currently available do not provide sufficient grounds for an effective education policy. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of a government bill amending the Law - Education Law and some other laws. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u403	Yes, sir. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, the bill aims to improve the quality of intra-Community rail transport. An institution will be introduced to pre-authorise the European Railway System Management System. Technical solutions will require the approval of the European Union Railway Agency. Railway infrastructure managers will have to apply for approval of the proposed solutions. The actual implementation of the single ERTMS system will allow inter alia. to increase capacity on existing railway lines, increase passenger safety, manage high-speed railway vehicles, harmonise the supply market for infrastructure managers and improve the punctuality of rail services. Documents relating to the safety of the rail system will also be amended. In this respect, a single safety certificate issued by the European Union Railway Agency shall be introduced. The President of the Railway Transport Office will be the competent authority for issuing this certificate for applicants operating only in the territory of Poland. Among the numerous changes contained in this bill, it is also worth paying particular attention to the obligation on carriers to support families and severely injured persons and their families. The carrier will be obliged to provide the victims with comprehensive information on the redress procedure and to provide psychological support at their own expense. The proposed law is fully applicable to the implementation of European Union law. The Republic of Poland has committed itself to implementing the proposed provisions, so Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the law in its entirety. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u405	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Mr. Marshal, how can we briefly talk about a brick like this? You can do it. This is an important bill. I'll tell you why. For decades, rail systems in European countries have developed independently and very different technical solutions have often been used, as the best example is even different rail spacing. This made it difficult and even prevented the provision of competitive transport services in a common railway area which had been built for some time. Competition in the transport market is becoming a reality through the European Union's regulations. However, the opening up of markets takes place gradually. This Law amending the Rail Transport Act implements two Directives of the European Parliament and of the Council: on the interoperability of the European Union rail system of 11 May 2016 and, with the same date, on safety. They are the foundation of the so-called. Fourth railway package. Interoperability is intended to ensure the technical compatibility of the rail systems of the different European Union countries. Within the framework of interoperability, we can distinguish structural subsystems that concern infrastructure, power supply systems, control systems, but also rolling stock. The authority which will be authorised to ensure interoperability requirements in Poland is the President of the Railway Transport Office. In this law, on which we are currently proceeding, we equip the President of the Railway Transport Office with numerous new competences. Greater competence is also the greater responsibility of the President of the UTK. It is the President of the UTK, when it comes to the area of the Republic of Poland, but also the European Union Railway Agency will issue uniform safety certificates. The Act sets out in detail the procedures for obtaining those certificates. The Civic Coalition supports the provisions of the Fourth Railway Package. Interoperability is an opportunity to reduce rail transport costs. The technical coherence of the rail sector is an opportunity to increase competition in rail transport, but also an opportunity to be used in inter-branch competition with road transport. Interoperability can also be used by Polish companies, Polish rolling stock manufacturers, infrastructure components, suppliers of power and control systems. It is also an opportunity for Polish carriers, because they can reduce operating costs by using modern rolling stock. If there is no need to replace locomotives with dual-system locomotives when there is no need to install locomotives which are approved, approved on the market of other countries, then there will be savings: saving time, saving money. Interoperability pays off, and let's add that we are talking about interoperability in rail systems. The railroad's a green transport, too. [[♪ The bell ♪]] healthy air and a better climate. Thank you. [[Applause]]

This draft law is the implementation of the obligation resulting from the incorrect transposition by the European Commission of Directive 2011/92/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 13 December 2011 on the assessment of the effects of certain public and private projects on the environment, i.e. so-called. Directive EIA, more specifically, Article. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 3 of that Directive. Reasoned opinion of the European Commission of 7 March 2019 pursuant to Article. 258 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union requires urgent action to address these shortcomings. The European Commission points out that the rules governing the adoption of decisions for projects covered by the EIA Directive, i.e. projects likely to have a significant impact on the environment, in some cases prevent recourse to a court for interim measures so that implementation of the project can be suspended and the resulting environmental damage prevented until it is determined whether those decisions have been adopted in accordance with the EIA Directive. These include, in accordance with the opinion of the European Commission, water law permits, geological and mining concessions, building permits and decisions issued on the basis of the so-called. specialty, which in Polish there are almost dozens. High Izbo! According to Polish law, the issue of permits for investments that are projects that can significantly affect the environment is basically two-stage. The first stage is the definition by administrative decision of the environmental conditions for the implementation of the project, and the second stage is the adoption of a decision granting the right to implement it. The introduction of general standards to the Environmental Impact Assessment Act for the correct transposition of the EIA Directive, with respect to the principles and procedures not related to this Directive, as set out in the Investment Process Act, with reference to these general standards, is the best and most effective solution to ensure consistency of national legislation. High Izbo! The draft law in question in the article. 1 amends the Act of 3 October 2008 on Environmental Impact Assessments, which are in Article. 72. 6 and 6a, i.e. the obligation to issue the decision of the public information body to publish decisions on environmental conditions relating to projects that may significantly affect the environment and the rules for making documents available. In turn, in the article. Article 85 (1) (b) and (c) of 3 there is an obligation to inform you of the possibility of reading the content of the decision, the case file, the arrangements and the opinions of the competent authorities. Added article. 86e-86h introduce the possibility, first, to suspend the immediate implementation of decisions on environmental conditions, second, to complain about decisions on environmental conditions under Article. 61 § 3 Laws on proceedings before administrative courts, as well as the possibility of appeal by an organic organisation fulfilling certain requirements from an investment permit preceded by a decision on environmental conditions. These are the most important changes made to the Environmental Impact Assessment Act of 3 October 2008. Art. The projects in question include amendments to the various laws, starting with the Law of 26 March 1982 on the integration and exchange of land in Article. 2, by the Law on Forests in Article. 3, Construction law in Article. 4, Atomic Law in Art. 5, Aviation law etc., I have very little time, so I will not calculate it, ending with the Act of 17 December 2020 on the promotion of electricity generation in offshore wind farms in Article. 25. The strike went off on February 10, 1971. The memory of terror and fear after the December massacre on the coast was very fresh, but the power after December did not learn anything. She thought once she pacified, killed, it would be quiet. The increase in food prices, because that is why the whole battle began, remained, and the lowest salaries were not raised, and all the promises of the newly elected First Secretary of the PZPR Edward Gierek turned out to be just a lie. And this fraud, another one, made it 130,000. employees of the Łódź light industry - fibrous fibres, weaver, from the finishing room - and mainly, in 80%, they were women, saw on their belts with payouts an amount lower by 200.300 zł than before. So it's not only that no one's raised their wages, they've been reduced. The machines were stopped at the Cotton Industry Plant. Julian Marchlewski. It was the women who stopped the machines and persuaded their fellow masters to do the same, because the machines in such plants were always the men's masterminds. And so it was on February 10th, and on February 14th in Łódź 55 thousand were already on strike. employees of the light industry with 33 workplaces. First they tried to disarm it on the spot, then a government delegation arrived - of course, military and militia taboons were gathered. But it didn't help. The strike lasted. Prime Minister Jaroszewicz arrived, he thought for sure someone like who, but he would disarm this rebellion. He did not disarm, in the cotton industry of Marchlewski, or in the Peacekeepers' plants. These brave girls said no, and Mr. Prime Minister as unspeakable returned to Warsaw. They didn't dare use the army and the militia, and the girls knew they couldn't leave the workplace. There was no strike on the street, and it was a victory. On 15 February, a decision by the Council of Ministers to return from 1 March to prices prior to December 1970 was announced late in the evening. The Łódź fibers won. I will read only the last sentence of this resolution: The Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the 50th anniversary of this victorious strike expresses respect and admiration for the courage, discretion and determination of Łódź fibroids - the brave and wise (Dawn) heroes of the Polish road to democracy and independence. We thank you after 50 years and remember. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u487	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on honoring Władysław Żeleński on the 100th anniversary of death, prints No. 937 and 941. On 19 February this year, the Marshal of the Sejm addressed the above parliamentary draft resolution, the draft of the club Law and Justice, to the Committee on Culture and Media for the first reading. Our committee, the Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering this project at its meeting on 23 February 2021 and after making minor amendments in cooperation between Members from all the clubs participating in this meeting and in cooperation with the Legislative Office, invites the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft resolution. I'll read this proposed project. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on honoring Władysław Żeleński on the 100th anniversary of his death. On 23 January 1921, Władysław Żeleński - composer, pianist, conductor and educator belonging to the most outstanding Polish musicians of the 19th century died in Cracow. Władysław Żeleński was born in 1837 in Grodkowice in Małopolska. He began his piano lessons with Jan Germasz and his music theory with Franciszek Mirecki in Cracow. He then went to Prague, where he studied organ playing, and also received a PhD from the University of Charles. Then he studied composition with Napoleon Henry Rebel at the conservatory in Paris. After returning to the country in 1872 he was a professor of harmony and counterpoint at the Music Institute in Warsaw and later - artistic director of the Warsaw Music Society. In 1888 he organized the Conservatory of the Music Society in Krakow and became his professor and rector. The composer's fame brought prestige to the university, which he led for 33 years. Władysław Żeleński is an artist whose achievements fit into the trend of national creativity, strengthening the spirit of Polishness in the years of captivity. His works are primarily operas referring to the Moniuszko tradition, such as Konrad Wallenrod, Old Fairy tale, Janek or Goplana, inspired by the works of national literature, and symphonic works derived from romantic music. Władysław Żeleński is a composer of symphony, overture in the Tatras, string quartets, piano trio and numerous other piano works and songs. Many of his works were printed in renowned publications in Germany and Italy. Although during the composer's life his works were highly appreciated and successful, today Władysław Żeleński's work was largely forgotten. Most of his works are currently difficult to access - many of his works are missing and modern restorations are rare. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to Władysław Żeleński on the 100th anniversary of his death, wishing to restore him a proper rank in the pantheon of national artists as an outstanding composer and representative of Polish neoromantic music. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u488	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear two minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I ask Mr Piotr Babinetz, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, to speak.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u491	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The creator is versatile, reaching to most genres and forms, reliable in crafts guardian of the composer's tradition, artist responsible for art, outstanding organizer of musical life in the country, successor of Stanisław Moniuszko. Such an image of Władysław Żeleński was recorded by outstanding musicologists associated with Krakow: Zdzisław Jachimecki and Józef Reiss. I am very pleased that it is a man associated with Małopolska and that it was me who took part in presenting the position of the Civic Coalition Club about a special, commemorative resolution on the 100th anniversary of the death of this outstanding man. He studied philosophy because of his mother. That's how his mother managed his life. But in Krakow it was very dynamic. St. Louis or setting the statue of Adam Mickiewicz on the market. He was an organist in the Basilica of Mary. ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u497	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media on the draft resolution on the celebration of Józef Brandt on the 180th anniversary of his birth, prints No. 938 and 942. As in the case of Władysław Żeleński - Władysław and not Stanisław Żeleński - the Committee on Culture and Media carried out its first reading, it considered this project at its meeting on 23 February this year. and after making minor amendments, he adds that the High Sejm should approve this draft resolution. And the draft resolution is: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the celebration of Józef Brandt on the 180th anniversary of his birth. On 11 February 1841 Józef Brandt was born - one of five, next to Jan Matejka, Piotr Michałowski, Józef Chełmoński and Henryk Siemiradzki, the most outstanding Polish painters of the 19th century. The artist was born in Szczebrzeszyn in a wealthy, Warsaw-based, socialized intelligent family with German roots. His grandfather was a professor of medicine at the University of Warsaw. The painter's father, also a doctor, fought in the November Uprising. He died prematurely of typhus, planting his family. First lessons of drawing Józef Brandt took from his mother, Krystyna from Lesslów - amateur painter, daughter of the famous architect Fryderyk Albert Lessl. In 1859 the future artist began engineering studies at the School of Bridges and Roads in Paris, but abandoned them for painting. He met Julius Kossak and was influenced by him to study in the historical painting studio of Léon Cognita. After returning to the country together with Julius Kossak, he traveled to Ukraine and Podole. From then on, the Eastern Kresas became his favorite subject. Brandt made his debut in 1861 at the exhibition of the Warsaw Society for the Encouragement of Fine Arts. Two years later, he began his studies at the Academy of Fine Arts in Munich, where he perfected his painting workshop, creating works in the field of realism and battle painting. In 1870 he opened his own studio in Munich, which belonged to the most famous in the city. He collected a collection of very valuable items: old weapons, furniture, and clothing, gathered with love and fame. He was considered the leader of the so-called. Munich School of Polish Painting, which included among others. Aleksandra and Maximilian Gierymski, Alfred Wierusz-Kowalski, Wojciech Kossak and Leon Wyczółkowski. He was an international success. For his accomplishments he was awarded and honored with prizes at many exhibitions. He became an honorary professor of the Academy in Munich and a member of the Academy of Arts in Berlin and Prague. The artist, even though he lived many years outside the country, always emphasized his Polishness, expressing it even by signing the paintings as Józef Brandt from Warsaw. He spent the last years of his life in his wife's estate in Orońska near Radom, where he died in 1915. His canvas restored the memory of the greatness and knightly past of the Republic of both Nations. He painted battles, skirmishes, driveways, genre scenes from the history of Poland - from the times of victorious wars with Turkey, Russia and Sweden. Such scenes can be seen, among others. in pictures: 'The Battle of Chocim', 'Czarniecki pod Koldynga', 'Battle of Vienna', and 'Departure from Wilanów Jan Sobieski with Marysieńka' and 'Barski Confederates'. His favorite themes included scenes depicting the colors of the lines, which are visible in the paintings: 'City on Podol', 'Nad Dniestr' or 'Cossack wedding'. He was happy to paint horses, hunts and fairs - he is the author of e.g. paintings such as 'Falk Targ', 'Jews leading horses to the market', 'Jarmark in the Balta in Podole', on the occasion of the presentation of the scenes of trade in horses, charts, falcons or eastern carpets, were portrayed by small nobles of Podolska and Oriental merchants. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the 180th anniversary of the birth of Józef Brandt - an outstanding Polish painter, honours his memory and expresses his appreciation for the artist's creative achievements and great contribution to saving Polish identity and national consciousness. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We proceed with the examination of item 22. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Energy Efficiency Act and some other laws (print No 957). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Climate and Environment, Mr Jack Odoba, to present the justification for the bill. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u509	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to present a draft amending the Energy Efficiency Act. At the outset, I will say that this is the implementation of the provisions of the 2018 Directive. The aim of the project is to create a framework for the new solutions provided for in the revised Directive in 2018 aimed at achieving the national final energy savings target at the end of 2030. The most important changes made to the Energy Efficiency Act include: modifications of the current legal framework of the energy efficiency certificates system by extending the catalogue of entities obliged to market liquid fuels for transport purposes, allowing obliged entities to fulfil their energy savings obligations in the form of non-refundable subsidies to connect customers to the district heating network or to exchange heat sources with final customers. The Act introduces the so-called. alternative measures as a complementary tool to the energy efficiency certification system. The Act also seals the system for monitoring and reporting energy savings by establishing a central register of final energy savings. The energy savings of alternative measures will be aggregated in the register. The next point we want to put forward in this proposal is the strengthening of the provisions on energy efficiency agreements. The Act clarifies the situations in which these agreements will not affect the increase in the level of public debt. In addition, the legislation contains an amendment to the Act - Energy law to strengthen the existing rules on measuring and individualising heat billing for final customers. Moreover, in addition, the amendment of the Act on personal income tax, the Act on Public-Private Partnership and the Act - Environmental Law. Importantly, the project does not entail a burden on the state budget and the budget of local government units. The project increases the revenues of the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u535	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! A few days ago we celebrated the 150th anniversary of the birth of Eugeniusz Romer - a Polish geographer, cartographer and geopoliticalist, educator, patriot, one of the most outstanding Poles of the last century. I wanted to remind you of this special Pole. Eugeniusz Romer was born on 3 February 1871 in Lviv. His parents Edward and Irena came from old noble families. He spent his childhood in Krosno. In 1881, he started his middle school in Jasła, and continued his studies in Nowy Sącz. After receiving the certificate of maturity he studied history, law, ethics, literature history, geography and geology at the Jagiellonian University, Halle and Lviv University. He graduated in 1893. A year later, he defended his PhD in philosophy and then passed his teacher's exam. He deepened his education in Vienna, Berlin. From 1899 he was a professor of the Lviv Academy of Commerce, in 1908 he became an extraordinary professor at the University of Lviv, where he obtained the title of professor of ordinary. He created a geography school in Lviv, including physical, economic and cartography. On the spectrum principle developed by him, the international scale of height colours developed. To describe the map he proposed mainly block writing, easy to read. Polish naming was used on Polish maps. In his 1904 edition of Geography of Polish Lands, he stated: Poland is a land representing a geographical whole, and this is the reason why, despite the political breakdown, it has not yet died. The concept of Poland is stronger than the temporary coincidence of historical facts and political changes. He fought for national identity, political subjectivity of Poland as an area, national and cultural community. His works gave a thorough, theoretical justification of the place of Poland in Europe. In 1908, a small geographical Atlas was released. Thanks to him, Poland's synthetic and school cartography was in the world's forefront. In 1916 he published the Geographical and Statistical Atlas of Poland, in which he presented the image of Poland in terms of physical, national, economic and cultural terms, showed where the Poles reached, where they were cultural, economic and organisational forces and where they were in minorities. It was published in four languages. He was a member of the Polish delegation at a peace conference in 1918 in Paris, organized after the end of World War I. During the conference the Treaty of Versailles was signed. In 1921 he participated in the justification of the topographical details of the Polish Eastern border. In 1921. [[♪ The bell ♪]] founded the Cartographic Institute of Atlas, in which maps and atlases were printed according to its concept. He founded the first cartographic magazine.Polish Cartographic Review. After the war, he worked to the end. He died on 28 January 1954. Let's remember the great Poles. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The policy of facilitating the placement of Polish textile craftsmen and dressers on the ground was an attempt to make effective use of the situation in which the Russian market was absorbent but closed to products from Europe. Acting in the spirit of activating residents of government cities, the Mazowieckie Voivodeship Commission led by Rajmund Rembielinski, wanting to encourage the settlement of Polish settlers, weavers and dressers from abroad, introduced a number of facilitations in the case of the opening of textile workshops. On 30 March 1821 a contract was signed, so-called. between the Provincial Commissioner of the Administrative Department of the Mazowieckie Voivodship Clement Witkowski and the representatives of the settlers: Carl Gottlieb Saenger from Chodzieza, Johann Heinrich Teske from Samocin and Johann Georgie Viertel from Rogózno. The agreement assumed that all new settlers, after taking the oath of faithfulness, acquired rights granted to citizens of the Kingdom of Poland. Each of them received a plot under the house and workshop and a large garden. The first immigrants were granted the right to harvest a tree free of charge for the construction of houses. The sons of the settlers were released from military service as soon as they learned the profession of weaver. The settlers have been granted the right to tax exemption for six years. Thanks to the Zgierz agreement, a modern, smartly designed settlement was created, whose fragments can still be admired in the Tkaczy City Cultural Park. The new town allowed for the rapid development of Zgierz, which over 10 years from a small town inhabited by 600 inhabitants became the economic leader of the region inhabited by several thousand people. The rules of the agreement were also in force in other towns of the then Mazowieckie voivodship: Dąbiu, Gostynin, Przecz or Łódź, which only several decades later became a world phenomenon of development and the power of light industry. But before that, Zgierz was the largest producer of dresses in this part of Europe. The Zgierska agreement is an example of the actions of the state authorities, which, thanks to tax reliefs, facilitating business placement and material support, gave a huge boost to development for local government and citizens. In a special jubilee year, the two hundredth anniversary of this important document, I wish the town of Zgierz all the prosperity, prosperity and continuous, wonderful development. Dear Compassion! Let's be proud of our town. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u550	Thank you, Member. And the last statement will be made by Andrzej Grzyb, the Coalition of Poland. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-02-24-sejm-26-1.u551	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I recall today the July 2020 summit, the European Council, after which the Prime Minister of the Polish Government, Mateusz Morawiecki announced that a major negotiation success had been achieved. In fact, if it were only the multiannual financial framework, we could not talk about the success of negotiations, but there was also a reconstruction plan, including a fund, which provides for almost EUR 60 billion for Poland. All local authorities, many local communities, of course, are very much looking forward to the distribution of this multi-annual budget and, in particular, what part of it will fall on regional operational programmes. And of course there is a big dissonance, because e.g. for Wielkopolska, the regional operational programme was allocated in this first division, in the first proposal only 44% the allocation from the previous financial perspective, i.e. a reduction of 56%. This is, Mr Ziejewski said here, almost twice as much as regards the reduction in relation to the previous financial perspective. We are aware that we are a region that has crossed this barrier 75% average GDP in the European Union by 1%, Lower Silesia by 2%. And now that we compare these potential proposals for divisions, including declarations made by government representatives, among others. in Lower Silesia, where it is said that Lower Silesia, which is in a similar situation as Wielkopolska, could potentially obtain additional funds from this 7-billion-dollar reserve, which will allow it to approach this limit of EUR 2.2 billion, i.e. Lower Silesia will have as much as in the previous financial perspective. Wielkopolska is in a similar situation, so we also expect an allocation that will come closer to this ratio of EUR 2.5 billion, which was in the previous financial perspective. Those declarations were made at a press conference with ministers from the current government, so I believe they are credible declarations. Therefore, we are concerned that there may be some dissonance in the distribution of money for regional operational programmes, and we also intervene as the Wielkopolska Parliamentary Team. This statement of mine today is also intended to serve, so to speak, the rational and fair distribution of money into regional development. The fact that the region is economically viable should not disqualify it in the allocation of European funds. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]
